TESS: The clinic's book is ash. This is all there is of it.

PEG: And the other edge of it.

TESS: Yes. *(flat)* Without the rolls, there's nothing to match Purfleet. Nor twenty-five year of anything. No tickets, no trial.

The stove ticks. Danny gets up, one hand doing the work of two, and opens the stove door. The firebox — never raked out since the night she burned them — holds a month of cold ash, and in the ash, curled edges: the ferry timetable's print, and the corner of a cloth binding.

DANNY: Burn them tonight. We lose the bridge. We stay free.

---

## Act five: The vote

### Scene 14. The weighbridge office, Sunday evening.

The same seats as every job night of the season. The stove door open on its cold ash. The boxes of rolls stacked ready, either for the van or the fire; nobody has said which. By the till, where he clerked all night, Kieran's exercise book lies open.

PEG: Round the table. Out loud, so we all hear it.

DANNY: Burn.

The word sits there. Danny looks at it — then down the table, to the open exercise book by the till. The second column. The one headed *me*. Thirty-nine two, thirty-eight nine, thirty-eight six, thirty-eight three, thirty-eight nought. A boy's careful pencil, going down all spring, in a house that never noticed.

Then at the boy.

DANNY: *(quietly)* No. Give.

KIERAN: Give.

ROSIE: Give.

SHAUN: Give.

Maggie doesn't say the word. She gathers her six tide books off the table, squares them, edges to edges, and lays them on top of the rolls. Then Joe puts his hand flat on them, once, and takes it away.

Peg is last. She looks at the open stove, at the certificate, at the photo of the big man laughing, at the bare board. Then she stands, and crosses the room, and shuts the stove door herself on the cold.

PEG: Give. *(beat)* Paper talks.

And she starts stacking the boxes herself, and nobody takes one off her.

### Scene 15. Grays police station, Sunday night.

Rain on the yard. The van at the front steps. Peg carries the boxes in, two at a time, seventy-two years old, and refuses the constable who comes to help. On top of the last box, flat and wrapped in its tea towel, lies a rectangle the size of a small window. At the van she set it there herself, saying so.

PEG: And this goes back on whatever wall it come off.

Inside, a DESK SERGEANT under strip lighting. The boxes go on the counter, and the tea-towel bundle on top. He lifts the bundle aside, gently, and unwraps a corner: a woman at a window, holding a little brass balance up to the light.

SERGEANT: And this is?

PEG: Stolen. Mayfair. March. *(beat)* Mind, her window's on the wrong side. She came back like that. She's staying like that.

Rosie stands in the doorway with her rig on her belt. The sergeant unclips it — belt, collar, wrist unit, the brass disc swinging on its strap — and writes her a property receipt on a pad. The second of her life. She folds it small, and puts it where the Obverse leaflet used to live.

Tess comes in last of all of them. She stops at the counter, and holds out her wrists.

TESS: Twenty-five year of jobs on them rolls, and every one since I was fourteen was planned off my sums. Every number's mine. Start with me.

PEG: *(to the sergeant, of the boxes)* They're in order. *(beat)* And their sea went down my drain. Write that an' all. *(beat)* And my girl stepped out on purpose. Twice. Put it with the rest.

He begins, unhurried, at the top of the first box.

---

## Epilogue. December

### Scene 16. The front page.

A train carriage, December dark outside. On a commuter's knee, a newspaper: **THE TEN-SECOND CURE — A GRAVEL FIRM'S RECORDS.** Inside, boxed for cutting out, the table: seconds down one side, odds down the other, printed for anyone. In a newsagent's window, a silent telly: OBVERSE SHARES HALVE. On the news, Dr Helen Osei, calm as ever, says one line — "That price fed forty thousand people." — and below it a second headline runs: REGISTER ASKS LEAVE TO OFFER CROSSING NEXT DOOR.

Over the headlines, a ward at the Royal Free. ROSIE, HAND: T on her badge, gowned and gloved for her patients' sake, walks unmasked between fever beds nobody else can walk between without a hood. A WARD SISTER passes behind her, laying a hand on the foot of a bed as she goes.

SISTER: Your file lives in our kitchen now, love. You eat where you work. *(beat)* And you sit your exams in the spring, like anyone else.

A theatre. A rig's collar over her gown. A child asleep. The clock on the wall. At her shoulder, a LICENSED SURGEON, masked, watching the clock and not her.

SURGEON: Her first. Count her.

She is there; then the space where she was, the nurses watching it; then she is back, and something small and dark drops into the steel dish a nurse holds out. Three seconds. Inside the limit she once called soft.

Her locker opens once, on the last shot of the scene: a water jug, the Fever Company card, brought with her from Whitechapel, and a trainee's logbook, open at its first page, one line filled in, in her own hand.

### Scene 17. Visiting day.

A prison visiting room. Tess in grey turns a letter over: from Kieran, a graph drawn on the back in pencil. She reads it twice, then folds it and puts it inside her shirt.

A Register ward, quiet. Danny, at a table, healed into a smooth pale plane across the knuckles of his one hand. No door on his room locked except the pantry's, down the corridor, and the little green light above it. On his table, an appointment card: LICENSED RESTORATION — MARCH. He turns it over. Sets it down. Turns it over again. He hasn't decided.

The same day, at both doors, Ray — charged with misconduct, waiting like everybody else for his hearing — signs both visitors' books, in plain, unhurried writing.

### Scene 18. Breakfast at the bridge.

Frost on the gravel heaps. Peg — out on bail for the depot, and for putting the other hand down a Grays drain — serves Kieran his fry-up, and waits while he eats every bite, and then points her spatula at the steel.

PEG: On the plate. Everything in you counts.

The printer chatters. She tears off the ticket and writes the number on the roll, and Kieran adds his pencil under the day's lorries: *checked against Maggie's.* Beside Frank's photo, newly framed, hangs Terry's last ticket — 70.6 — and under it, in Peg's hand: *71.4 on Maggie's. Went next door.* Sixpence, grey about the muzzle, lies where Terry's feet used to be at the stove.

Across the river, in the Ahern kitchen, Siobhan steps up onto a bathroom scale, hands up like a diver. Declan leans to the dial with his back to us, squints a long time, then straightens and chalks the board, and the room cheers. Under the scale, folded, lies December's front page, unread. Maggie stands in the doorway with the tide book under her arm, and does not cheer.

### Scene 19. The hook.

The office, evening. The radio talks into the warm dark.

RADIO: ...the gravity group at Reading say the estuary tremors which began in November spell a second molecule. This time, a sugar of our own hand — and the signal, they say, is fainter than the first.

The SCIENTIST, mild and astonished on a riverbank: "It's ours. We don't know why."

Kieran, at the counter, writes one word on the roll: *fainter.* Peg, at the stove, looks up at Terry's ticket in its frame, a long time.

**Last shot:** the Grays outfall at Nobody low water, the whole estuary running quietly out to sea.

**Cut to black.**

**END OF EPISODE 10**

---

## Plants and payoffs

**Paid off here, from episodes 1 to 9**
- **"Fifteen stone when the black took him" (1, 3, 4), under the painted rule a fifth of a second a kilo under a hundred (1):** the third reversal. The box marked 02 (7), Frank's falling tickets with Peg's own pencil on them, Terry's "a man that can't eat", and the jam jar (1) replayed as it was — with Rosie in the hatch wearing the same look. **Farringdon, September, four seconds (named here; one in twelve on Tess's lips)** is the turn the family misread as weight. "Your dad never had a second to spare in his life" (3) and "A tenth was always plenty" (5) close on *7.1* — said by Peg, over her own pencil.
- **Ray's "Tomorrow, then" and "sixty-four kilos" (9), his shut 2002 folder (8):** the wall's month, 64.3 and 7.1 seconds in the black — read off the wall's own log, not Peg's ticket — "At seven he was still at the tray. Starving men are slow", and "I wrote it all down and showed nobody."
- **The drawer (1, 4, 5, 9):** the Ministry's 2001 letter, opened at last, by Peg, with the line that kept it shut for twenty-five years read aloud twice. "There's your number, Frank" pays "There's your number, love" (9).
- **Tess's pencil (1–9):** comes down from behind her ear and goes into Kieran's pocket. **"Paper talks. Burn it." (1)** becomes "Paper talks" over boxes bound for Grays police station — and Tess's claim at the counter ("Every number's mine. Start with me.") is why she is the one inside in December.
- **Tess's "It was a different week" (1, 2):** she tells Kieran which week, at the night of writing, in the words she told Rosie on the office step.
- **The cargo rule, the ferry count, the loft, "we feed our own" turned inside out (7, 8):** the night of writing puts names to the ferry sheet on certified paper, with Maggie's tide books and Ray's own file as the other people's paper. **The clinching exhibit:** a hidden turning is a falling year on the family's rolls, and the Register's claim dates can name every one.
- **The tug lore (1), Rosie's sea under Grays (9), the receipt "found wet, sand adhering" (1), Frank's tide saying (1), Maggie's tables (7):** the door, found on a baking tray with a ruler propped level, water tipped down to Nobody low, and a salt pot. The small key on orange baler twine (5, 6) opens the gate nobody changed in thirty-one years.
- **The bite (5, 9):** blind, lightless, gaugeless — it brings Rosie home from their shore and costs Terry nothing he can see.
- **The lying plate and the steelyard (9):** two numbers for Ray (8.4 and 8.3), a 7.5 with the margin kept — "She still lies. We know it now" — and the minus ticket, torn off only after he's home, printing him true. Kieran's two numbers, 38.0 and 38.4, side by side.
- **Rosie's practised plain hand (3) and her mirror cards (1, 2):** Ray's cure in one stroke — eleven years of writing back to front coming out mirrored now he's straight — with the meter, never a look, settling it: 5.1.
- **Terry's five years, his eleven hops, his pot, his gloves:** "Terry. Turned. 2021", said and written at the table; the die and "Six, I stay hungry. Anything else, I get my tea"; the gloves refused to the handshake and folded on the gate rail; the washing-day song's half line, and then, at the gate, "One. Two." and no goodbye; and the two routes weighed before he chooses — "Five seconds, six goes" against one go next door.
- **Terry's last ticket (1, epilogue):** printed at last — 70.6 on the little bridge, torn off by Kieran and folded into Terry's breast pocket, where the die goes; Peg finds it with his clothes at the gate, and it is what she frames beside Frank.
- **Rosie's vow (7: "when it's been counted proper... I'm going first"):** answered at the fence — seven in ten weighed against the wards, "It's wrote down now. It'll keep" — in the plan's own words for the choice itself. **Her surgeon videos (1, 2) and Den's dressings (8):** the three-second tumour, inside the limit she called soft, with a licensed surgeon at her shoulder — "Her first. Count her." — and her first line filled in, in her own hand, in a trainee's logbook; the Sister's exams line pays "Marlows don't do paper" (2). **The recruiter's card (8):** pocketed at Whitechapel and brought with her, and in December the ward sister's line — "Your file lives in our kitchen now" — is the sentence that holds her there. **Liam's star and 65.1 (9):** the board wiped bare at last. **The property receipt (5):** the second of Rosie's life. **The painting:** given back on purpose, with its turned window named — "She's staying like that."

**Left open, and planted for a second season**
- **The second message (began November, a sugar of our hand, fainter):** readable four ways, and the season has shown every rule each reading needs — they can feed us; they have found our life in their sea; someone arrived the wrong way round; or it comes from further off, from the Far, where some of the lost went. The Register is asking leave to cross next door.
- **Whether Terry arrived straight or turned.** Nobody looks, nobody is shown, and the camera stays with the family.
- **What Rosie saw, and what her last breath was like** — released alone, twenty yards off, into our air.
- **What went down the Grays drain in June**, still sitting under Peg's bail.
- **Danny's card for March, turned over and over.** **Shaun in the queue, waiting to be counted.** **Tess's appeal, and Kieran's graph, drawn on the back of a letter.** **The outfall at Nobody low water** — where the sea that owns them runs out, and where season two waits.

**Numbers used.** Canon only: 78.0, *12 st 4*, 64.4, *10 st 2*, *7.1*, 64.3, 7.1 seconds in the black, Farringdon September 2001, four seconds (one in twelve), seven seconds, "a tenth"; 89.0, 71.4, 52.5, 58.0 and 8.4, 58.6 and 8.3, 7.5, about four in ten, 5.1, −58.6; 46.2; 38.0 and 38.4; 70.6; five and a bit, one in six, five in six, one in twelve in four, one in two and a hand; 7 in 10; three seconds along the Hill; eight metres; 1995, thirty-one years; 05:52; fourteen; 2021; eleven years; 2019. Derived, nothing resting on them: the intermediate tickets of Frank's curve (76.9 down through 66.9); the tingle at 6.3 (two seconds short of Ray's true 8.3); the depot's "nine times" (eleven cases less Shaun's two). No new canonical numbers. New dialogue numbers, derived from the canon table and logged: six goes of five seconds, two in three (1 in 6 each go); the retry before the warrant.
